Idea: Define a "good" predicate on terms via a measure of "redex potential" that strictly decreases. Consider the following classic measure for STLC: define the "size" of a term where abstractions and boolean constants have size 1? No wait.

Let's think: In CBV STLC with Bool and if, every reduction step is:
1. (λ.t) v --> t[v] where v is a value.
2. t1 t2 --> t1' t2 when t1 --> t1' and t2 lc.
3. v1 t2 --> v1 t2' when t2 --> t2'.
4. if true t1 t2 --> t1; if false t1 t2 --> t2.
5. if t1 t2 t3 --> if t1' t2 t3.

A well-founded measure: Consider assigning to each term a natural number "weight" w(t) such that w decreases at every step. If we can find such a w, SN follows immediately (well-founded induction on nat). This would be much easier than logical relations!

Does such a w exist? For STLC (simply typed), the standard proof of SN can be done by assigning to each typed term an ordinal / natural via the "hereditary" interpretation. But without types, no. We need a type-directed measure. We can define w_T(t) for t : T by recursion on T and the term:

- w_Bool(t) = some count of "steps to normal form bound"? For booleans: terms of type Bool include if-expressions, applications returning Bool (with function type), etc. Hmm.

Idea: Use a "size of the normal form + number of steps" bound? For simply typed lambda calculus, the maximum reduction length is bounded by a function of the term (e.g., using the "hereditary substitution" or the standard "weight" where each variable occurrence contributes the weight of its substituted value, leading to towers of exponentials). A classic measure for STLC SN (Girard's proof uses ordinals; but for a simple syntactic measure, we can use "the interpretation in the free algebra of monotone functions"?).

Let me think carefully and design the proof from scratch, being precise.

Approach: reducibility with *values* and *evaluation contexts*.

We want to prove: for closed well-typed t, SN t.

Define two predicates by recursion on type T:
- `R_T(t)` ("t is reducible"): for all terms t', if t -->* t' and t' is a value... hmm.

Standard CBV Tait-style:

Define:
- `V_T(v)` for values: 
  - `V_Bool(v) := v = true \/ v = false`
  - `V_{T->U}(v) := forall w, V_T(w) -> R_U (v w)`  (and maybe lc)
- `R_T(t) := forall v, t -->* v -> value v -> V_T(v)`? No, that's weak (only normalizing, not SN). For SN we need more.

### Basic lemmas about open_rec and lc

We need:
1. `lc_at_mono` / weakening: if lc_at k t and k <= k', then lc_at k' t. Useful.
2. `lc_open_rec`: if lc_at (S k) t and lc_at k u then lc_at k (open_rec k u t)? Let's see: opening zaps bvar k with u. In abs case, k -> S k, need lc_at (S k) u, from lc_at k u by weakening. Good.
3. Conversely, `lc_open_rec_inv`: if lc_at k (open_rec k u t) and lc_at k u then lc_at (S k) t? Hmm, might not be true in general? In bvar case, if t = bvar i, open gives u (i=k); lc_at k u, need lc_at (S k) (bvar k), true. If t = bvar i, i ≠ k: result bvar i; lc_at k (bvar i) gives i < k < S k. Good. In abs case: lc_at k (open_rec k u (abs T t1)) = lc_at k (abs T (open_rec (S k) u t1)) implies lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) u t1); apply IH with premise lc_at (S k) u (weakening), get lc_at (S (S k)) t1, then lc_at (S k) (abs T t1). Fine. So inversion lemma holds.

5. Opening with fvar x then substituting: We need a substitution operation for free variables, because the fundamental theorem needs to replace fvars by terms. Define:

```
Fixpoint subst_tm (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app ... 
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst_tm sigma t1)
  | ...
  end.
```

### Definitions

```
Fixpoint R (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t =>
      locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (tm_app t v))
  end.
```

Wait: should R_Bool be SN only or SN + lc? Include lc. And for the value clause in arrow, should we quantify over values only? Let's double check the fundamental theorem application case with this definition:

Fundamental: Gamma |-- t \in T -> rho ~ Gamma -> R T (subst rho t).

- T_App case: t = tm_app t1 t2, t1 : T1->T2, t2 : T1. IH1: R_arrow(subst rho t1). IH2: R_T1(subst rho t2). Need R_T2 (app (subst rho t1) (subst rho t2)) = R_T2(subst rho (tm_app t1 t2)) since subst distributes. Use application lemma:
  `app_lemma: forall T1 T2 t1 t2, R (T1->T2) t1 -> R T1 t2 -> R T2 (tm_app t1 t2)`.
  This we prove separately, by induction on SN t2 and SN t1 as discussed. Let's think about how to prove this cleanly.

Actually, we can define R_arrow with a stronger clause over all terms (not just values) *and* prove it closed under expansion, but the abs case needs R_T2((λ.t1) v) which we get by expansion from R_T2(open t1 v). That works! Let's define:

```
R_Arrow T1 T2 t := lc t /\ SN t /\ (forall t2, R T1 t2 -> R T2 (tm_app t t2))
```

Then application is trivial. But the abs case now needs, for the clause "forall t2, R T1 t2 -> R T2 ((λ.t1) t2)": we know R_T2(open t1 t2) for all t2 in R_T1? From IH instantiated with rho[x:=t2]: need t2 arbitrary (not just value) and the fundamental theorem gives R_T2 (subst rho' (open t1 (fvar x))) = R_T2(open (subst rho t1) t2) if x fresh. But we need R_T2((λ.(subst rho t1)) t2), which is an expansion of open (subst rho t1) t2 by a beta step — but ST_AppAbs requires value t2! Ah, here's the crux: if t2 is arbitrary (not a value), then (λ.t1) t2 does NOT step to t1[t2]; instead it reduces by evaluating t2 first. So expansion along beta only works for values t2.

Therefore the arrow clause must quantify only over *values* t2, and then the application lemma for arbitrary t2 must be proven by induction on SN t2 (evaluating t2 to a value). That's the standard CBV structure. So R_arrow = lc ∧ SN ∧ (forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (t v)). The application lemma (forall t2, R T1 t2 -> R T2 (t1 t2)) needs the "evaluate the argument" induction on SN t2, plus handling t1 stepping by induction on SN t1.

Standard "termination" logical relation for CBV (from Ahmed's notes):
- `V[[Bool]] = {true, false}`
- `V[[T->U]] = {λx.e | ∀ v ∈ V[[T]]. e[v/x] ∈ E[[U]]}`
- `E[[T]] = { e | ∃ v. e ⇓ v ∧ v ∈ V[[T]] }` — but this gives weak normalization/termination, not strong.

- `S[[Bool]] = SN`
- `S[[T->U]] = { e | SN e ∧ ∀ v ∈ V[[T]]. e v ∈ S[[U]] }` where V[[T]] ⊆ S[[T]] are values.

**Alternative: Normalization by evaluation / hereditary substitution?** Since the language has no fixpoints, maybe we can define a function that computes the normal form and prove reduction preserves it? Hmm, SN requires all paths finite; a deterministic step relation: if we show each step decreases a well-founded measure, done. Since step is deterministic, t is SN iff there's no infinite chain. But is the strong arrow definition even well-formed as a Fixpoint? 
```
Fixpoint R (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Bool => fun t => lc t /\ SN t
  | T1 -> T2 => fun t => lc t /\ SN t /\ (forall t2, R T1 t2 -> R T2 (tm_app t t2))
  end.
```
This is a plain Fixpoint on T with R T1, R T2 for smaller types; structurally decreasing. Fine. But note: the requirement "forall t2, R T1 t2 -> R T2 (t t2)" includes t2 stuck etc. With the stuck lemma, the abs case handles stuck t2. **T_Abs case** (fundamental theorem): 
Context Gamma, types T1, T2, body t1. Given Hbody: forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2.

Components:
1. lc: need lc (abs T1 (subst rho t1)). We know from typing that the original abs is lc (has_type -> lc), i.e., lc_at 1 t1. Wait, lc_at doesn't restrict fvars, so substituting an fvar with a non-lc term could break lc_at. So need sigma x lc_at k. 2. SN (abs T1 (subst rho t1)): any abs is SN (no steps). Trivially by SN_intro: for all t', step (abs) t' -> SN t'; no steps. Need a lemma `step_abs_inv` or `value_SN`. Let's try yet another angle: strengthen the arrow relation to avoid the "value clause at arrow results" problem by defining R via a *fixpoint over SN derivations*, i.e., define the relation by well-founded recursion on the SN derivation, allowing R_arrow(t) to mention R_arrow(t') for reducts. Then maybe the abs case's value clause can be handled without case splits? Let's see: Define R_T(t) with the strongest natural clause:

R_Bool(t) := lc t ∧ SN t
R_{T->U}(t) := lc t ∧ SN t ∧ forall t', step t t' -> R_{T->U}(t') ∧ forall t2, R_T t2 -> R_U(t t2)

Note: this is a "coinductive-like" definition but well-founded on SN derivations. If we define this as an inductive predicate:

```
Inductive R : ty -> tm -> Prop :=
| R_Bool : forall t, lc t -> SN t -> R Ty_Bool t
| R_Arrow : forall T1 T2 t, 
    lc t -> 
    (forall t', step t t' -> R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t') ->
    (forall t2, R T1 t2 -> R T2 (tm_app t t2)) ->
    R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t
```

Positivity: R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t' occurs positively (to the right of arrows). `R T1 t2 -> R T2 (app t t2)`: R T1 t2 occurs negatively! Strict positivity fails. Unless we encode the negative occurrence as a "positive" via defining a separate inductive family for "R at each type" via a Fixpoint over ty producing an inductive? Can't define inductives inside Fixpoints.

Hmm, but there's a standard trick: define the relation as an inductive family indexed by the SN derivation? Ugh.

Actually, here's a classical and simple measure for STLC SN (I recall now): define for each term t its "maximum reduction length" by recursion using a *type-indexed* interpretation into the natural numbers where applications get bounded by iterated exponentials. Specifically, for a term t : T, define:

- `sz(t)` = number of nodes.
- For each type T, define a "norm" ||T|| such that if t : T then the number of steps is bounded by something like f(||T||, sz(t)).

The Tait-style proof is essentially the standard route; let's not reinvent.

Let's also double check the `R_if` lemma's use of expansion:

Actually, there's a well-known issue: Coq's automatically generated induction principle for an inductive family where a recursive argument is under a binder works fine (it's just a product). The generated scheme should be:

```
has_type_ind :
  forall P : context -> tm -> ty -> Prop,
  (forall Gamma x T, Gamma x = Some T -> P Gamma (fvar x) T) ->
  (forall L Gamma T1 T2 t1,
      (forall x, ~ In x L -> P (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2) ->
      P Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (T1 -> T2)) ->
  ...
  forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> P Gamma t T
```

Yes, Coq generalizes the recursive occurrence under the forall x and the ~In condition. I'm fairly confident.

Let's now also verify `step_trichotomy` will be used inside R_app_abs and R_if. Its proof needs lc_at inversion lemmas at level 0 and step reasoning. Let's write it.

```
Lemma step_trichotomy : forall t,
  locally_closed t ->
  value t \/ (exists t', step t t') \/ (~ value t /\ ~ (exists t', step t t')).
Proof.
  induction t; intros Hlc.
  - (* bvar n *) inversion Hlc as [k i Hi | | | | | |]; subst. (* lc_bvar *) 
    (* Hlc : lc_at 0 (tm_bvar n) gives n < 0 *)
    lia.
  - (* fvar *) right; right; split; [intros Hv; inversion Hv| intros [t' Hs]; inversion Hs].
  - (* app t1 t2 *) 
    inversion Hlc as [k a b Ha Hb | ...]; subst. (* should give lc_at 0 t1, lc_at 0 t2 *)
    destruct (IHt1 Ha) as [Hv | [[t1' Hs] | [Hnv Hns]]].
    + (* t1 value *) 
      destruct (IHt2 Hb) as [Hv2 | [[t2' Hs2] | [Hnv2 Hns2]]].
      * (* both values: check t1 shape *)
        destruct t1; try (inversion Hv); ...
      ...
```

Hmm, this is getting verbose but manageable. Let's think about a smarter formulation. Maybe prove the trichotomy via a boolean decision procedure? That might actually be cleaner in Coq: define `step_dec : forall t, {t' | step t t'} + {forall t', ~ step t t'}` requiring no lc? Hmm, but step requires lc premises; without lc, terms can be weird. Trichotomy proof structure:

```
Lemma step_trichotomy : forall t, locally_closed t ->
  value t \/ (exists t', step t t') \/ (~ value t /\ ~ (exists t', step t t')).
Proof.
  induction t as [n | x | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH1 | | | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 t3 IH3];
    intros Hlc; try (inversion Hlc; subst).
  - (* bvar *) left? no: lc_at 0 (bvar n) gives n < 0; lia.
  - (* fvar *) right; right; split.
    + intros Hv; inversion Hv.
    + intros [t' Hs]; inversion Hs.
  - (* app *) 
    (* Hlc gives lc t1, lc t2 after inversion *)
    destruct (IH1 H0) as [V1 | [[t1' S1] | [NV1 NS1]]].
```

- other shapes contradict value.

Now let's think about the R definition and whether `Fixpoint` with the strong clause typechecks structurally: 

```
Fixpoint R (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t =>
      locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall t2, R T1 t2 -> R T2 (tm_app t t2))
  end.
```

R T1 and R T2 are recursive calls on immediate subterms of T; accepted. ✓. (Note: R is a function returning Prop; the recursive calls are on T1, T2, structurally smaller. ✓)

Let me now also consider whether the fundamental theorem needs the `subst_lc` condition `forall x, lc (rho x)`; ctx_ok as:

```
Definition ctx_ok (Gamma : context) (rho : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (rho x)) /\
  (forall x, locally_closed (rho x)).
```

In T_Var: need R T (subst rho (fvar x)); subst rho (fvar x) = rho x; apply Hctx's first component. ✓
In T_Abs: need lc(abs T1 (subst rho t1)) — from has_type_lc (abs T1 t1) and subst_lc (forall x, lc(rho x)) ✓.
In T_True: R Bool true = lc true ∧ SN true ✓.

Hmm, do we need `subst_lc` at level 0 or general k? For the abs body: subst rho (abs T1 t1) = abs T1 (subst rho t1); lc of that requires lc_at 1 (subst rho t1). So need subst_lc at level 1 from lc_at 1 t1 and forall x, lc_at 1 (rho x) (from lc_at 0 rho x by monotonicity). General lemma:

```
Lemma subst_lc_at : forall rho k t, lc_at k t -> (forall x, lc_at k (rho x)) -> lc_at k (subst_tm rho t).
```
Proof by induction on t (or on lc_at derivation); the abs case increases k to S k: need forall x, lc_at (S k) (rho x), from lc_at k (rho x) by monotonicity. ✓.

Similarly, we might need `subst_lc` at 0 only in T_Abs via abs. Fine.

Now the fresh-atom machinery. Since atoms are nat and context is a function, freshness only matters for the list L in T_Abs and FV of t1. Let's define free variables:

```
Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => []
  | tm_fvar x => [x]
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => []
  | tm_false => []
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3
  end.
```

Note: under a lambda, fvars of the body are free; bvars ignored. ✓.

Lemmas:
- `subst_ext : forall rho1 rho2 t, (forall x, In x (fv t) -> rho1 x = rho2 x) -> subst_tm rho1 t = subst_tm rho2 t.` By induction on t.
- `fv_open`: relates fv of open with fv. We need: x ∉ fv t1 → for all y, In y (fv t1) → y ≠ x. That's by definition: In x (fv t1) <-> ... We need a lemma `fv_correct : forall t x, In x (fv t) <-> occurs_free x t`? Maybe not needed; for subst_ext, we need rho1 and rho2 agree on fv t1. We have rho' y = if eqb x y then v else rho y. For y ∈ fv t1, need y ≠ x. We know ~ In x (fv t1) (from freshness). So y ∈ fv t1 and y = x would give In x (fv t1) by rewriting. So we don't need full fv correctness, just the list membership: `~ In x (fv t1)` and `In y (fv t1)` imply y ≠ x. ✓.

Wait, but there's another subtlety: we need rho' ~ update Gamma x T1 to apply the IH. rho' = fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then v else rho y. For y = x: update Gamma x T1 x = Some T1 (need eqb x x = true and the update definition: `update Gamma x T1 y = if Nat.eqb x y then Some T1 else Gamma y`; at y=x, Some T1 ✓). rho' x = v ∈ R T1 ✓. For y ≠ x: update Gamma x T1 y = Gamma y (since eqb x y = false); Hctx gives R (Gamma y) (rho y); rho' y = rho y ✓.
Also need forall y, lc (rho' y): at x, lc v from value; else lc(rho y) ✓.

Then the IH: `IHbody x HxL rho' Hctx'` gives R T2 (subst rho' (open t1 (fvar x))). Commutation: subst rho' (open t1 (fvar x)) = open (subst rho' t1) (rho' x) = open (subst rho' t1) v. And subst rho' t1 = subst rho t1 by subst_ext with the agreement on fv t1. So R T2 (open (subst rho t1) v). ✓.

Note: `open t u` is `open_rec 0 u t`; commutation lemma at k=0: subst rho' (open_rec 0 (fvar x) t1) = open_rec 0 (subst rho' (fvar x)) (subst rho' t1) = open_rec 0 v (subst rho' t1). ✓.

Now the choice of x: we need x with ~ In x L and ~ In x (fv t1). Use fresh_exists on (L ++ fv t1): get x with ~ In x (L ++ fv t1). Then ~ In x L and ~ In x (fv t1) by `in_app_iff`. ✓.

Fresh exists for nat lists:

```
Fixpoint max_list (l : list nat) : nat :=
  match l with
  | [] => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Lemma max_list_ub : forall l x, In x l -> x <= max_list l.
Proof. induction l; simpl; intros; [contradiction|]. destruct H as [->|H]; [apply Nat.le_max_l | apply Nat.le_trans with (max_list l); [apply IHl; auto | apply Nat.le_max_r]]. Qed.

Lemma fresh_exists : forall l : list nat, exists x, ~ In x l.
Proof. intros l. exists (S (max_list l)). intros Hin. apply max_list_ub in Hin. apply Nat.nle_succ_diag_l. (* S m <= m -> False *) 
```
`Nat.nle_succ_diag_l : forall n, ~ S n <= n`. So `exact (Nat.nle_succ_diag_l _ Hin)`. ✓.

Alternatively use `exists (fold_right ...)`. max_list works.

But careful: `step_lc_back : step t t' -> lc_at 0 t' -> lc_at 0 t`. Induct on the step derivation. For ST_AppAbs: t = app (abs T body) v, t' = open body v. Premises: lc(abs) and value v. Need lc(app) = lc(abs) ∧ lc v. We have lc(abs) from the rule premise ✓, and lc v from value ✓. No need for inversion of t'! Great, ST_AppAbs is trivial.
For ST_App1: t = app t1 t2, t' = app t1' t2; premise t1 --> t1', lc t2. H: lc_at 0 t' → lc t1', lc t2. IH t1 --> t1' with lc t1' gives lc t1. Then lc(app). ✓
For ST_App2: t = app v1 t2, t' = app v1 t2'; premises value v1, t2 --> t2'. H gives lc v1, lc t2'. lc v1 from value (redundant). IH t2 --> t2' with lc t2' gives lc t2. ✓
For ST_IfTrue: t = if true t1 t2, t' = t1. H: lc t1. Premises: lc t1, lc t2. Need lc(if true t1 t2): lc true, lc t1, lc t2. ✓
ST_IfFalse similar.
ST_If: t = if t1 t2 t3, t' = if t1' t2 t3; premises t1 --> t1', lc t2, lc t3. H gives lc t1', lc t2, lc t3. IH gives lc t1. ✓

`step_lc_fwd : step t t' -> lc_at 0 t -> lc_at 0 t'`. Induct on step:
- ST_AppAbs: t = app(abs T body) v, t' = open body v. Premises: lc(abs) [which is lc_at 1 body], value v. Need lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 v body): apply lc_open_rec with k=0: need lc_at 0 v (value) and lc_at 1 body (from lc(abs), which we have from the rule premise directly, no need to use lc t!). ✓
- ST_App1: t' = app t1' t2; need lc t1' (IH with lc t1 from lc t) and lc t2 (rule premise). ✓
- ST_App2: t' = app v1 t2'; need lc v1 (value) and lc t2' (IH with lc t2 from lc t). ✓
- ST_IfTrue: t' = t1; lc t from lc(if true t1 t2) gives lc t1. ✓
- ST_IfFalse: similar.
- ST_If: need lc t1' (IH), lc t2, lc t3 (rule premises). ✓

`lc_open_rec : lc_at k t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t)` — wait, the level: opening at level k replaces bvar k; body under abs uses k+1. But if i > k, then i < S k holds (i = k? excluded, so i < k or i > k? if i < S k then i ≤ k; i ≠ k → i < k ✓). Good. So the lemma is:

```
Lemma lc_open_rec : forall k t u, lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t).
```

And the inversion:

```
Lemma lc_open_rec_inv : forall k j t u, j <= k -> lc_at k (open_rec j u t) -> lc_at k u -> lc_at (S k) t.
```

Now let's also consider `subst_open_rec`: needed for the commutation. Let's define subst_tm:

```
Fixpoint subst_tm (rho : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => rho x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst_tm rho t1) (subst_tm rho t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst_tm rho t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst_tm rho t1) (subst_tm rho t2) (subst_tm rho t3)
  end.
```

Commutation:
```
Lemma subst_open_rec : forall rho k u t,
  subst_tm rho (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst_tm rho u) (subst_tm rho t).
Proof. intros rho k u t. revert k u. induction t; intros k u; simpl; auto.
  - (* bvar *) destruct (Nat.eqb k n); auto.
  - (* abs *) specialize (IHt (S k) u); simpl in *; rewrite IHt; auto.
  ...
```
Hmm, induction on t with k and u generalized. ✓.

`subst_ext`:
```
Lemma subst_ext : forall t rho1 rho2,
  (forall x, In x (fv t) -> rho1 x = rho2 x) ->
  subst_tm rho1 t = subst_tm rho2 t.
Proof. induction t; simpl; intros; auto. ... 
```
For app: rewrite IHt1, IHt2 with appropriate In_app facts. Use `in_app_iff`. For if: triple app. For abs: IHt with fv unchanged.

Lemma R_step : forall T t t', R T t -> step t t' -> R T t'.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; intros t t' HR Hstep.
  - (* Bool *) simpl in HR. destruct HR as [Hlc Hsn].
    simpl. split.
    + apply step_lc_fwd with (t := t); auto.
    + apply SN_step with (t := t); auto.
  - (* Arrow *) simpl in HR. destruct HR as [Hlc [Hsn Happ]].
    simpl. split; [apply step_lc_fwd with t; auto|]. split; [apply SN_step with t; auto|].

```coq
(** * Auxiliary definitions and lemmas for the strong normalization proof *)

(* Fresh atoms *)
Fixpoint max_list (l : list nat) : nat :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Lemma max_list_ub : forall (l : list nat) (x : nat),
  In x l -> x <= max_list l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| a l IH]; intros x Hx; simpl in *.
  - destruct Hx.
  - destruct Hx as [-> | Hx].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; eauto | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_exists : forall (l : list atom), exists x, ~ In x l.
Proof.
  intros l. exists (S (max_list l)).
  intros Hx. pose proof (max_list_ub l _ Hx). 
  exact (Nat.nle_succ_diag_l _ H0).
Qed.
```

Check names: `Nat.nle_succ_diag_l : forall n : nat, ~ S n <= n`. Let me verify. Also `Nat.le_max_l : forall n m, n <= Nat.max n m`. Yes.

Hmm, `Nat.nle_succ_diag_l` might be named `nle_succ_diag_l`. Let's check quickly through a test.

Free vars:

```coq
Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3
  end.
```

Lemma R_stuck : forall T t,
  locally_closed t ->
  (forall t', ~ step t t') ->
  ~ value t ->

  - simpl. split; [exact Hlc|]. split.
    + apply SN_intro. intros t' H; exfalso; apply (Hns t'); exact H.

      apply IH2.

Lemma R_app_abs : forall T1 T2 s,
  locally_closed (tm_abs T1 s) ->
  (forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (open s v)) ->

    + apply Hsub; auto.
  - (* step *) apply R_expand with (t' := tm_app (tm_abs T1 s) t2').
    + apply ST_App2; [apply v_abs; auto | auto].

    + intros Hv; inversion Hv.
Qed.
```

Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T Hty tm_fvar) as HR.
  - (* ctx_ok empty tm_fvar *)
    split.
    + intros x U Hx. unfold empty in Hx. discriminate.
    + intros x. apply lc_fvar.
  - (* HR : R T (subst_tm tm_fvar t) *)
    rewrite subst_identity in HR.
    apply R_sn in HR. exact HR.
Qed.
```

  locally_closed t ->
  value t \/ (exists t', step t t') \/ (~ value t /\ ~ (exists t', step t t')).

Now the trichotomy itself. Let me write it with these helpers:

```
Lemma step_trichotomy : forall t,
  locally_closed t ->
  value t \/ (exists t', step t t') \/ (~ value t /\ ~ (exists t', step t t')).
Proof.
  induction t as [n | x | a IHa b IHb | T body IHbody | | | c1 IHc1 c2 IHc2 c3 IHc3];
    intros Hlc.
  - exfalso. apply lc_at_bvar_inv in Hlc. lia.
  - right; right; split.
    + intros Hv; inversion Hv.
    + intros [t' Hs]; inversion Hs.
  - (* app *)
    apply lc_at_app_inv in Hlc as [Hla Hlb].
    destruct (IHa Hla) as [Hva | [[a' Hsa] | [Hnva Hnsa]]].
    + (* a is a value *)
      destruct a as [n0 | x0 | a1 a2 | T0 body0 | | | a1 a2 a3];
        try (exfalso; inversion Hva).
      * (* a = abs T0 body0 *)
        destruct (IHb Hlb) as [Hvb | [[b' Hsb] | [Hnvb Hnsb]]].
        -- right; left; exists (open body0 b). apply ST_AppAbs; assumption.
        -- right; left; exists (tm_app (tm_abs T0 body0) b'). 
           apply ST_App2; [apply v_abs; assumption | assumption].
        -- right; right; split.
           ++ intros Hv; inversion Hv.
           ++ intros [t' Hs]. exact (step_app_abs_stuck T0 body0 b t' Hla Hnvb Hnsb Hs).
      * (* a = true *)
        destruct (IHb Hlb) as [Hvb | [[b' Hsb] | [Hnvb Hnsb]]].
        -- right; right; split.
           ++ intros Hv; inversion Hv.
           ++ intros [t' Hs]. exact (step_app_val_stuck_arg tm_true b t' v_true Hnvb Hnsb Hs).
        -- right; left; exists (tm_app tm_true b'). apply ST_App2; [apply v_true | assumption].
        -- right; right; split.
           ++ intros Hv; inversion Hv.
           ++ intros [t' Hs]. exact (step_app_val_stuck_arg tm_true b t' v_true Hnvb Hnsb Hs).
      * (* a = false *) same
    + (* a steps *) right; left; exists (tm_app a' b). apply ST_App1; assumption.
    + (* a stuck *) right; right; split.
      + intros Hv; inversion Hv.
      + intros [t' Hs]. exact (step_app_stuck_fun a b t' Hnva Hnsa Hs).
  - (* abs *) left. apply v_abs. assumption.
  - (* true *) left. apply v_true.
  - (* false *) left. apply v_false.
  - (* if *)
    apply lc_at_if_inv in Hlc as [Hl1 [Hl2 Hl3]].
    destruct (IHc1 Hl1) as [Hv1 | [[c1' Hs1] | [Hnv1 Hns1]]].
    + destruct c1 as [n0 | x0 | a1 a2 | T0 body0 | | | a1 a2 a3];
        try (exfalso; inversion Hv1).
      * (* true *) right; left; exists c2. apply ST_IfTrue; assumption.
      * (* false *) right; left; exists c3. apply ST_IfFalse; assumption.
      * (* abs *) right; right; split.
        -- intros Hv; inversion Hv.
        -- intros [t' Hs]. exact (step_if_abs_stuck T0 body0 c2 c3 t' Hs).
    + right; left; exists (tm_if c1' c2 c3). apply ST_If; assumption.
    + right; right; split.
      * intros Hv; inversion Hv.
      * intros [t' Hs]. exact (step_if_stuck_fun c1 c2 c3 t' Hnv1 Hns1 Hs).
Qed.
```

Note: the destruct order for tm: tm_bvar, tm_fvar, tm_app, tm_abs, tm_true, tm_false, tm_if. The pattern `[n0 | x0 | a1 a2 | T0 body0 | | | a1 a2 a3]` ✓.

Issue: `inversion Hva` to derive contradiction in non-value shapes: for a = tm_bvar n0, Hva : value (tm_bvar n0); inversion closes. For a = tm_fvar, app, if: same. With `try (exfalso; inversion Hva)` — for value shapes (abs/true/false) this tactic fails? exfalso changes goal to False; inversion Hva might not close for value shapes; the `try` will leave the goal changed to False if inversion doesn't close! `try (exfalso; inversion Hva)` — if the inner tactic sequence partially succeeds (exfalso succeeds, inversion fails), the whole sequence fails and `try` leaves the state after exfalso? In Coq, `try tac` where tac fails: it reverts to the state before tac? `try` is a tactic combinator: if tac fails, it does nothing (state unchanged, goals restored). Partially applied tactics inside a compound `tac1; tac2` — if tac2 fails, the whole `tac1; tac2` fails, and `try` restores? Tactic failure semantics in Ltac: `try (t1; t2)` — if the compound fails, try succeeds with the original goal unchanged (since the tactic expression failed, the state is rolled back). I believe Ltac is transactional to some extent: `try` catches the failure and the goal is unchanged. Yes, `try (exfalso; inversion Hva)` when Hva is a value shape: inversion Hva doesn't fail (it succeeds generating 3 subgoals maybe?), hmm. Actually `inversion Hva` on value (tm_abs ...) will produce the v_abs case (and v_true/v_false don't unify), leaving a goal. So `exfalso; inversion Hva` might leave a goal `False` with hypotheses, which is bad: the branch would remain as a goal. Hmm.

But in the destruct a cases, the non-value shapes are bvar, fvar, app, if; for these, inversion Hva yields zero subgoals (no constructor unifies) → closes. So `exfalso; inversion Hva` closes. For value shapes (abs, true, false), we handle them explicitly, so we don't apply the try tactic there (the `try` is attached to each destruct branch, not to the whole destruct). Actually the syntax `destruct a as [...]; try (exfalso; inversion Hva).` applies the try to ALL branches, including abs/true/false. In those branches, `exfalso; inversion Hva` might leave a False goal — but then we continue with the `*` bullets? Hmm, the structure:

```
      destruct a as [...] ; try (exfalso; inversion Hva).
      * (* abs *) ...
```
If the `try` leaves an unsolved goal in the abs branch (after exfalso+inversion), then the abs bullet would face a False goal, and our subsequent tactics would fail. Hmm. Actually would `inversion Hva` on `value (tm_abs T0 body0)` leave a goal? inversion Hva generates the v_abs constructor case: with premises `locally_closed (tm_abs T0 body0)`; the goal after exfalso is False. Inversion does not close it. So yes, it would leave a goal. That's bad.

Better: use `try (exfalso; inversion Hva; fail)`? Or restrict: for the non-value shapes, `exfalso; inversion Hva` closes; for value shapes we want to keep the original goal. Use `try solve [exfalso; inversion Hva]`: `solve` requires the tactic to fully solve the goal; if not, the whole solve fails and try leaves the goal unchanged? `try solve [t]` — if t doesn't solve, solve fails, try does nothing (goal unchanged). Yes! `try solve [exfalso; inversion Hva]` is the right construct: only applies if it fully solves.

Alternatively, use `try discriminate` style. Let me use `try solve [exfalso; inversion Hva]`.

Hmm, for a = tm_bvar n0: exfalso; inversion Hva. Does inversion on value (tm_bvar n0) close? The value constructors: v_abs : value (tm_abs T t); v_true : value tm_true; v_false. Unifying tm_bvar with tm_abs/tm_true/tm_false fails → inversion closes the goal (inversion proves False by discriminate). I believe inversion closes when no constructor unifies. Yes.

The trichotomy compiles. Now let's add the logical relation R and the associated lemmas. Let me write:

```
Fixpoint R (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
               (forall t2, R T1 t2 -> R T2 (tm_app t t2))
  end.

Definition ctx_ok (Gamma : context) (rho : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  (forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (rho x)) /\
  (forall x, locally_closed (rho x)).

Lemma R_lc : forall T t, R T t -> locally_closed t.
Proof. induction T as [| T1 IH1 T2 IH2]; simpl; intros t H; tauto. Qed.
```

Lemma R_app_abs : forall T1 T2 s,

```
Lemma has_type_lc : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T -> locally_closed t.
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty as [Gamma x T Hx
                 | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHbody
                 | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 Ht1 IH1 Ht2 IH2
                 | Gamma
                 | Gamma
                 | Gamma t1 t2 t3 T Ht1 IH1 Ht2 IH2 Ht3 IH3].
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - apply lc_abs.
    destruct (fresh_exists L) as [x Hx].
    pose proof (IHbody x Hx) as Hlc.
    apply (lc_open_rec_inv 0 0 t1 (tm_fvar x)); [lia | exact Hlc | apply lc_fvar].
  - apply lc_app; assumption.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
  - apply lc_if; assumption.
Qed.
```
Careful: the induction principle for has_type in the T_Abs case: the pattern gives Hbody and IHbody? From the printed induction principle:
```

      (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...) ->
      (forall x, ~ In x L -> P (update ...) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2) ->
      P Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (T1 -> T2)
```
So the pattern should be `[L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHbody]`. ✓ (two arguments: the typing premise and the IH).

Now the fundamental theorem:

```
Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall rho, ctx_ok Gamma rho -> R T (subst_tm rho t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty as [Gamma x T Hx
                 | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHbody
                 | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 Ht1 IH1 Ht2 IH2
                 | Gamma
                 | Gamma
                 | Gamma t1 t2 t3 T Ht1 IH1 Ht2 IH2 Ht3 IH3];
    intros rho [HrhoR HrhoLc].
  - (* T_Var *) simpl. apply HrhoR. exact Hx.
  - (* T_Abs *) simpl.
    destruct (fresh_exists (L ++ fv t1)) as [x Hx].
    assert (HxL : ~ In x L) by (intro H; apply Hx; apply in_app_iff; left; exact H).
    assert (Hxfv : ~ In x (fv t1)) by (intro H; apply Hx; apply in_app_iff; right; exact H).
    assert (Hlc1 : lc_at 1 t1).
    { apply (lc_open_rec_inv 0 0 t1 (tm_fvar x)); [lia | | apply lc_fvar].
      apply has_type_lc with (Gamma := update Gamma x T1) (T := T2). apply Hbody; exact HxL. }
    assert (Hlcabs : locally_closed (tm_abs T1 (subst_tm rho t1))).
    { apply lc_abs. apply subst_lc_at with (k := 1).
      - exact Hlc1.
      - intros y. apply lc_at_weaken with (k := 0); [lia | apply HrhoLc]. }
    split.
    + exact Hlcabs.
    + split.
      * apply value_SN. apply v_abs. exact Hlcabs.
      * intros t2 HR2.
        apply R_app_abs with (T1 := T1) (T2 := T2) (s := subst_tm rho t1).
        -- exact Hlcabs.
        -- intros v Hv HRv.
           specialize (IHbody x HxL (extend_rho rho x v)).

           assert (Hok' : ctx_ok (update Gamma x T1) (extend_rho rho x v)).

             - intros y U HyU. 
               destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
               + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y.
                 unfold update in HyU. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl in HyU.
                 injection HyU as ->. apply extend_rho_same. ... 
```

Now the T_App case:
```
  - (* T_App *) simpl.
    apply (proj2 (proj2 (IH1 rho (conj HrhoR HrhoLc))) (subst_tm rho t2)).
